EEF1AKMT2 methylates the translation elongation factor eEF1A 57, 58 , a core component of protein synthesis. Expression, protein abundance, default mode connectivity, and educational attainment thus rise together, which links the regulation of protein synthesis to this circuit and to cognition (Fig. 7F).

To find candidate drug targets, we audited each locus-gene-trait chain for five links: circuit closure, SuSiE-confirmed colocalization, an effector gene or colocalized brain protein QTL, a causal estimate, and a clinical-stage compound in Open Targets 59 . The audit was extended with cell type-specific and PsychENCODE expression QTL 50, 51 and GWAS of migraine, headache, and ischemic stroke (Methods; Table S9 and Table S10). Among the 150 chains of the 247 loci, the causal link passed for 27 (Fig. 7A), including RHEBL1 with cognition, GTDC1 with cognitive performance, and YPEL3 with worry. The cortical areas affected by all discovery loci and by the APOE, VRK2, FADS, EEF1AKMT2, and DPP4-region loci are mapped in Fig. 7G.

Three genes stood out for brain disorders. APOE was supported for Alzheimer's disease by colocalization at rs429358, the protein-altering ε4 isoform, colocalization with the ApoE protein QTL in cerebrospinal fluid (PPH4 = 0.9998), and circuit closure. It also has a direct therapeutic link: APOE2 gene therapy 60 has completed a phase 1/2 trial in ε4 homozygotes (LX1001; NCT03634007), and anti-amyloid antibodies act on the amyloid pathway downstream of APOE 61, 62 (Table 1). At the FADS locus, new to fMRI genetics and colocalized with bipolar disorder 41 , the variant shared with connectivity raised FADS1 expression in oligodendrocytes and cortex 52 and lowered disease risk. The same allele weakened connectivity in the cingulo-opercular and somatomotor networks and shifted plasma fatty acids toward the products of desaturation, arachidonic and omega-3 fatty acids (Supplementary Note). This chain links polyunsaturated fatty acid synthesis to connectivity and to bipolar disorder. FURIN, a proprotein convertase at 15q26.1, was also supported for bipolar disorder, and its higher expression predicted lower risk. FADS1 and FURIN are tractable drug targets 59 , and their protective direction of effect points to activation. RHEBL1 is also tractable, and its higher expression went with higher cognitive scores and lower ADHD risk.

Beyond these genes, 155 genes within 500 kb of the loci are targets of clinical-stage compounds (Table 1). Compounds of 50 of them had been tested for psychiatric disorders, and 15 were targets of approved psychiatric drugs, including SLC6A4 (antidepressants), HTR2A (antipsychotics), and CACNA2D2 and CACNB3 (pregabalin for anxiety). For seven genes, the compounds had been tested for the trait that colocalized with the locus. At the 10q25.3 locus, new to fMRI genetics and colocalized with ADHD, SLC18A2 is the target of amphetamines approved for ADHD. At an MHC locus colocalized with major depressive disorder, TNF inhibitors (infliximab and golimumab) have been tested in depression. For nine genes at cognition loci, compounds had been tested for cognitive impairment, including the DPP4 inhibitor sitagliptin at 2q24.2. Brain expression data also tied three drug targets to their loci: CRHR1 and MAPT at 17q21.31, colocalized with educational attainment, and MAPKAPK5 at 12q24.12, colocalized with reaction time.

Comparison of resting-state and task-state connectivity.

To see how brain state shapes genetic effects, we compared resting and task fMRI edge by edge (Fig. 8A and Fig. S2). Task connectivity came from a shorter scan and, because it was computed over all conditions of a block-design task, combines background connectivity with stimulus-evoked coactivation 16, 63 . Differences between the states therefore reflect measurement as well as brain state. Nearly all edges were heritable in both states, but resting heritability was higher: 39.9% of resting and 3.8% of task edges had h2 ≥ 10% (Fig. S2A, B), and resting heritability exceeded task heritability at 66.9% of the edges. The two heritability maps were weakly correlated (r = 0.10; Fig. 8A). Averaged over the connections of each network, resting heritability was higher in all 12 networks, most markedly in association networks (Fig. S2C). For connections within the somatomotor and auditory networks, task heritability was higher (7.3% versus 4.4% and 8.2% versus 2.7%; Fig. S2D).

Genetic discovery followed the same pattern (247 resting and 96 task loci; Fig. S2E). Of the 96 task loci, 28 lay within 500 kb of a resting locus, five with identical lead variants. Shared variants affected far fewer connections during the task: rs429358 (APOE) affected 589 resting and 149 task edges, and the EPHA3 variants affected 2,867 to 2,889 resting and 257 to 279 task edges (Fig. S2F). During the task, most of these footprints shifted toward the visual cortex. The emotion-processing task engages this cortex strongly, so stimulus-evoked coactivation is expected there.

Links to complex traits also differed between the states. Resting connectivity had more significant genetic correlations than task connectivity for schizophrenia (0.50% of edges versus none) and intelligence (1.45% versus 0.07%), whereas task connectivity had more for ADHD (0.58% versus 0.02%; Fig. S2G, H). Mendelian randomization gave FDR-significant estimates for 7 of 14 outcomes with the resting loci and none with the task loci (Fig. S1 and Table S11). Across the 21 traits tested at all loci (Methods), 129 locus-trait pairs at 65 of the 247 resting loci colocalized strongly, compared with 15 pairs at 9 of the 96 task loci (Fig. 8B; task loci in Table S12). Resting-state connectivity is thus more heritable than task-state connectivity, yields more loci, and colocalizes more often with complex traits.

Phenotypic associations with circulating molecular traits showed the opposite pattern. We tested each edge for association with 49 clinical biomarkers, 2,922 plasma proteins, and 251 nuclear magnetic resonance (NMR) metabolomic measures in the UKB. The Bonferroni threshold was P < 0.05/(M eff × K), where K is the number of features in the layer (Methods). Task connectivity had 37.1-fold more protein-edge, 13.4-fold more metabolite-edge, and 3.6-fold more clinical biomarker-edge pairs than resting connectivity (Fig. 8C, D and Table S13). Fatty acid composition and triglyceride-rich lipoproteins led the metabolomic associations (Fig. 8E). The protein associations differed between the states (Fig. 8F), and peak expiratory flow led the biomarkers in both (Fig. 8G). During the task, the metabolite associations extended to most of the connectome; omega-6 fatty acids, for example, were associated with 14,034 resting and 50,679 task edges (Fig. 8H and Table S14). The edges of the leading protein in each state, COL15A1 at rest and ACY1 during the task, are mapped in Fig. 8I.

The metabolite associations agreed between the states connection by connection. Of the resting edges with a metabolite association, 83% also had one during the task, and for each leading fatty acid measure, the direction agreed at 99.8% to 100% of the shared edges. Each measure had the same sign at nearly all of its edges (at least 95% of them for 167 of the 169 task measures). The associations shared by the two states were stronger during the task (median |Z|, 7.2 versus 5.2), even with a smaller sample (median n, 22,449 versus 26,236). Associations of one sign across most of the connectome fit a whole-brain component of connectivity that reflects systemic physiology, including the vascular contribution to BOLD coupling 64, 65 . In this account, the coactivation evoked by a block-design task amplifies the component. Task-state connectivity is thus less heritable than resting-state connectivity but more closely coupled to circulating metabolic traits, in line with a larger state-dependent component of connectivity during a task 66.

DISCUSSION

We present a connection-level genetic atlas of the human cerebral cortex in two brain states. The atlas comprises summary statistics for all 64,620 connections, 247 resting loci (193 new to fMRI genetics) and 96 task loci with their footprints, and edge-level genetic correlation maps for 36 traits. Three findings stand out. First, genetic effects on connectivity are largely connection-specific: most loci affected few edges, and most acted on connections between networks. The coupling between networks is itself heritable, and traits that aggregate connections average out this signal. Second, genetic predispositions to higher intelligence and to schizophrenia are associated with higher and lower default mode connectivity, respectively, and the 2q24.2 locus near DPP4 acts on the connections that carry the polygenic signal of intelligence. Its footprint centers on the left posterior temporal area PHT, and the right PHT is a recurrent hub of genetic effects; both are priorities for functional studies. Third, the APOE ε4 allele lowers connectivity across the posterior default mode and secondary visual networks, and this circuit effect persists after adjustment for mean connectivity. Its direction matches the connectivity changes reported in ε4 carriers and across the Alzheimer's disease spectrum 7, 45, 46 . The default mode connections of this footprint carry the genetic correlation of Alzheimer's disease with connectivity and are candidate imaging phenotypes for genetic and clinical studies.

Circuit closure, which underlies the second and third findings, revealed two kinds of link between a locus and the polygenic signal of its trait. At APOE, one locus carries the genetic correlation between Alzheimer's disease and connectivity: removing the APOE region from the disease GWAS abolished the genetic correlation at the footprint connections. Near DPP4, the 2q24.2 locus acts on the same connections as the polygenic signal of intelligence, in both samples and independently of its own contribution to the map. The first kind places a known genetic correlation on specific connections; the second points to circuits shared by a locus and the polygenic signal of its trait.

At the molecular level, the loci point to candidate mechanisms. For EEF1AKMT2, at a locus known from earlier fMRI GWAS 22 , higher expression and protein abundance (measured in induced pluripotent stem cells) were linked to higher connectivity and educational attainment. This sequence implicates the regulation of translation elongation. FADS1, at a bipolar disorder locus new to fMRI GWAS, links fatty acid desaturation to cingulo-opercular and somatomotor connectivity and to disease risk, in agreement with Mendelian randomization linking arachidonic acid synthesis to bipolar disorder 67.

The drug-target analysis links the loci to existing compounds and to new candidates. Within 500 kb of the loci, 155 genes are targets of clinical-stage compounds (Table 1). For seven of them, compounds had been tested for the trait that colocalized with the locus: amphetamines, which act on SLC18A2, are approved for ADHD, and TNF inhibitors have been tested in major depression. These links are positional and mark compounds for repurposing studies that first confirm the gene. For three drug targets, CRHR1 and MAPT at 17q21.31 and MAPKAPK5 at 12q24.12, brain expression data also tied the gene to the locus. Among the effector genes, APOE has a direct therapeutic link through APOE2 gene therapy. Tractable genes with genetic support point to new targets: higher RHEBL1 expression went with higher cognitive scores and lower ADHD risk, and at two bipolar disorder loci the protective direction of effect of FADS1 and FURIN calls for activation 68 . A complete chain from circuit to drug will need compounds for these genes and gene-level evidence for the existing drug targets.

These findings come mainly from resting-state connectivity, which shares a group-level architecture with task-state connectivity 69, 70 but differs from it genetically. The same variants affected fewer connections during the task, mainly in the visual cortex that the task engages 18 . With its higher heritability and more frequent disease colocalization, resting connectivity is the better substrate for genetic discovery. Task connectivity, in turn, had stronger and more widespread associations with circulating metabolites, and each metabolite's associations had the same sign across most of the connectome. This pattern points to a whole-brain, state-dependent component of connectivity that reflects systemic physiology, which makes task connectivity informative for studies of physiological state. Jointly modeling the two states may separate stable from state-dependent genetic influences.

The LDR framework made the edge-level analysis feasible and localized genetic effects to the connections that whole-brain ICA [20] [21] [22] and aggregated parcellation traits combine. For example, the 2q13 locus near PAX8, also detected with ICA 22 , affected 24,888 edges, whereas the VRK2 locus affected 53 edges, mostly in the default mode network. Researchers can use the atlas to place loci and traits on specific connections and to choose imaging endpoints. As samples grow, circuit closure and target chains can be tested for new loci with independent footprints and leave-locus-out maps.

Several considerations frame these results. Edge-level effects are reconstructed from 300 principal components per part, which explained 78% to 80% of the edge variance in each part. The basis retains a median of 88% of an effect that spans all edges of an area. Of an effect confined to a single edge, it retains about 2%, and 53% to 70% of the remainder falls on edges that share an area with that edge. Footprints therefore resolve genetic effects at the level of areas and their connections (Supplementary Note). The analyses used one parcellation and the autosomes, cell-type enrichment used annotations of four adult cortical cell types, and colocalization was tested at the top edge of each locus. The replication sample reproduced the sign of 99.6% of the study-wide significant effects, and in circuit closure, independent footprints and leave-locus-out maps controlled for the contribution of a locus to the map it was compared with. The task loci await replication in an independent task fMRI sample. The task data came from a single emotion-processing task 18, 71 , and task connectivity includes stimulus-evoked coactivation and is less reproducible and heritable than resting connectivity 20, 63 . Participants were middle-aged to older adults of European ancestry, and UK Biobank volunteers are healthier than the general population, and the associations with circulating molecular traits describe phenotypic co-variation in a cross-sectional sample. Larger, more diverse datasets with multiple tasks will show whether these results generalize across ages and ancestries.

In summary, the atlas created here moves the genetics of cortical functional connectivity from aggregated traits to individual connections. It places loci for cognition and disease on specific circuits, distinguishes the stable genetic architecture of resting connectivity from the state-dependent component of task connectivity, and provides summary statistics with which these circuits can be tested in new samples.

AUTHOR CONTRIBUTIONS

S.W. performed the data analyses, with contributions from T.L., Z.J., A.H., Z.S., X.L., S.G., S.L., S.Y., X.Q., I.P., Y.C., Y.J., B.T., Z.Z., S.H., Y.Z., P.G., M.H., and Y.D. contributed to UKB imaging data processing. S.W. and B.Z. wrote the manuscript. H.Z., B.Z., T.L., Y.L., J.S., and P.S. critically revised the manuscript.

Edge-level genome-wide association analysis.

Association analyses were performed with HEIG 28 , separately for resting and task fMRI and for four parts of 16,155 edges. Principal component analysis (PCA) of the mean-centered edges of each part in unrelated participants gave the bases (eigenvectors), onto which the connectivity of each participant was projected to construct LDRs, adjusting for age, age 2 , sex, their interactions, the top 40 genetic principal components, and assessment centre. This step used the functional PCA module of HEIG with kernel smoothing disabled, which is equivalent to standard PCA. The top 300 LDRs of each part were tested for association with 7,847,434 imputed autosomal variants (UKB imputation version 3; GRCh37) by linear regression with leave-one-chromosome-out adjustment for relatedness. After removal of non-SNP and strand-ambiguous variants, 6,633,888 SNPs remained, and the association of each SNP with each edge was reconstructed from the LDR summary statistics. The effective number of independent edges was computed from the eigenvalues λ of each part as M eff = (Σλ) 2 /Σλ 229 and summed over the parts (46.7 for resting and 26.9 for task fMRI). Study-wide significance was defined as P < 5 × 10 -8 /M eff . Software versions and further details are given in the Supplementary Note.

Imputed variants were retained when the minor allele frequency was ≥ 0.01, the imputation INFO score ≥ 0.8, variant missingness ≤ 10% and Hardy-Weinberg equilibrium P ≥ 1 × 10 -7 , and participants were retained when genotype missingness was ≤ 10%.

Definition of loci and replication.

Study-wide significant variants were retained when the number of edges with P < 1 × 10 -5 exceeded a null quantile estimated by HEIG. They were clumped with PLINK 77 using the 1000 Genomes European reference panel (lead variants at r 2 < 0.1 within 500 kb), within and across the four parts. Lead variants within 500 kb of each other were merged into one locus. The top edge of a lead variant was the edge with its smallest P value within the part in which the locus was identified. Its footprint comprised its study-wide significant edges, and circuit closure used its associations with the edges that had genetic correlation and heritability estimates (61,933 to 61,936 of the 64,620 edges). In the replication sample, connectivity was projected onto the discovery bases and analyzed in the same way. A locus replicated at the nominal level when its lead variant was associated with its top edge at P < 0.05 with the same direction of effect, and at the Bonferroni level when P < 0.05/247. Long-range LD proxies and the conditional analysis of the 17q21.31 region with GCTA-COJO 78 are described in the Supplementary Note.

Comparison with earlier GWAS. The lead variants were compared with the genome-wide significant associations (P ≤ 5 × 10 -8 ) in the GWAS Catalog (release 2026-08-24) and with the loci reported in earlier fMRI GWAS [20] [21] [22] [24] [25] [26] , counting associations listed in supplementary tables when they reached the significance threshold of their own study. A locus was considered previously reported when an fMRI association lay within 500 kb of its lead variant (Supplementary Note).

Heritability and genetic correlation.

Edge-level SNP heritability and genetic correlations with 36 complex traits (Table S15) were estimated with HEIG from the LDR summary statistics, using LD matrices of 1,159,563 HapMap3 variants estimated in 50,000 unrelated UKB participants of European ancestry. Heritability was tested at an FDR of 5% across edges. Genetic correlations allowed for sample overlap, and significance was defined as P < 0.05/M eff /37 for all traits, a Bonferroni correction for the 37 GWAS tested, of which liver disease had no valid SNP-heritability estimate (Supplementary Note).

Functional enrichment.

Enrichment analyses used the genome-wide summary statistics of one edge per locus, the edge with the most study-wide significant variants within 250 kb of the lead variant (228 distinct edges for the resting and 95 for the task loci). Because LD score regression is linear in the χ 2 statistic, each state was analyzed as the mean χ 2 across its edges, with block-jackknife standard errors. Stratified LD score regression 32 with the baselineLD v2.2 model 79 tested the ATAC, H3K27ac, and H3K4me3 peaks of neurons, astrocytes, oligodendrocytes, and microglia 33 , each against the union of the four, and estimated the fold enrichment of the union in a model with the baselineLD annotations. It also tested the specifically expressed genes of 54 GTEx tissues, each against all expressed genes 55, 80 . MAGMA 34 gene statistics were combined across edges (Table S16) and tested against 10,626 MSigDB gene sets 81 . Details are given in the Supplementary Note.

Colocalization. Colocalization between connectivity and 23 complex traits and diseases (Table S17) was tested with coloc.abf 35 (p1 = p2 = 1 × 10 -4 and p12 = 1 × 10 -5 ) in windows of ±500 kb around each lead variant, at the top edge of the lead variant. Pairs were tested when at least 50 variants were shared and the external GWAS had a regional association (P < 1 × 10 -4 ), and strong colocalization was defined as PPH4 ≥ 0.8 and PPH4 > PPH3. Strong pairs of the resting loci were refitted with SuSiE-based colocalization 36 (L = 5), with LD from the 1000 Genomes European reference panel 82 . For Alzheimer's disease, we used the consensus meta-analysis of Alzheimer's disease and related dementias without proxy cases 39 . Coronary atherosclerosis and liver disease were tested at the 112 replicated resting loci and the task loci, and the other 21 traits at all loci. Sensitivity analyses of the prior and of the tested edge are described in the Supplementary Note.

Multi-omics association analysis.

Edge-level associations with 49 clinical biomarkers, 2,922 plasma proteins (Olink), and 251 NMR metabolomic measures were estimated with the LDR framework in unrelated participants of European ancestry with imaging and omics data, adjusting for the covariates of the association analyses. Significance was defined as P < 0.05/(M eff × K), where K is the number of features in the layer, and features measured in fewer than 1,000 participants were excluded (Supplementary Note).

Mendelian randomization. Two-sample Mendelian randomization was performed with TwoSampleMR 83 , using the lead variants of the resting or task loci as instruments and GWAS of 14 cognitive, psychiatric, neurological, and sleep-related traits as outcomes (Table S18). The association of each instrument with connectivity was taken from the edge with the most study-wide significant variants near its lead variant. We report inverse-variance weighted (fixed-effect and multiplicative random-effects), MR-Egger, weighted median, and weighted mode estimates, with FDR correction across the 14 outcomes in each state (Supplementary Note).

Circuit closure. For each locus-trait pair with strong colocalization and a usable edge-level genetic correlation map (114 pairs at 59 loci for 18 traits), the footprint of the lead variant (-log 10 (P) at each edge) was compared with the genetic correlation map of the trait (-log 10 (P)) by the Pearson correlation over the edges with estimates. Null models that permuted the areas within each of the 12 functional networks 30 or rotated the area centroids on the sphere and reassigned them one to one [84] [85] [86] were too liberal, because footprints share edge-level structure with the maps (Supplementary Note). Each pair was therefore compared with all other discovery loci that did not colocalize with the trait (PPH4 < 0.5; outside the extended MHC region, chromosome 6 at 25-34 Mb, GRCh37; the two long-range LD proxies of the EPHA3 locus excluded), giving an empirical P value and a z score. P values were adjusted with the Benjamini-Hochberg procedure across all pairs. For each trait with at least three colocalized loci (PPH4 ≥ 0.8, outside the MHC), the closure of the colocalized loci was compared with that of the other loci by a one-sided Mann-Whitney test, with Benjamini-Hochberg adjustment across traits. Sensitivity analyses used SuSiE-confirmed loci only or partialled edge SNP heritability out of both maps. The pair and class tests were repeated with footprints re-estimated in the replication sample from its LDR summary statistics with the discovery bases; hubs in that sample used edges at P < 10 -5 . For the APOE-Alzheimer's disease pair and 15 other pairs (Table S7), the genetic correlation maps were re-estimated after removing the variants within 1 Mb of the lead variant from the trait GWAS. At the APOE locus, footprint edges with nominally significant genetic correlations with Alzheimer's disease (P < 0.05) were compared with the other edges by Fisher's exact test. The principal footprint hub of a locus was the area with the most study-wide significant edges; the number of loci per hub was compared with that expected from nodal SNP heritability (mean heritability of the incident edges) by a Poisson model with Pearson overdispersion (Supplementary Note).

Effector genes. At the 66 loci with at least one strong colocalization, the connectivity signal was fine-mapped with SuSiE, and the credible-set variants were annotated with Ensembl VEP 87 . A gene was placed in Tier 1 if it carried a protein-altering credible-set variant (posterior inclusion probability ≥ 0.1 in both the connectivity and the trait fits; predicted deleterious, or a missense variant classified in ClinVar 48 as pathogenic, likely pathogenic, or a risk factor). It was placed in Tier 2 if its expression QTL in any of 20 brain datasets of the eQTL Catalogue 49 colocalized with connectivity (coloc.abf screen and SuSiE confirmation). A gene was assigned when it was the only gene in the highest tier at its locus; ties were resolved by protein-coding biotype and then by a margin of at least 0.10 in the expression QTL PPH4. The assignment was repeated in five settings of fine-mapping and expression QTL screening, and with expression QTL of eight brain cell types, PsychENCODE, and MetaBrain as additional Tier 2 sources (Supplementary Note).

Expression-and protein-level causal analyses.

Cis-eQTL Mendelian randomization was performed for the 15 Tier 2 effector genes in the 13 brain tissues of GTEx v8 55 , with Steiger-filtered instruments 88 and Benjamini-Hochberg adjustment across all estimates. For each Tier 2 gene, SMR also used the top cis-eQTL (P < 5 × 10 -8 ) in each of the 20 brain datasets of the eQTL Catalogue; a dataset supported the causal link when the Benjamini-Hochberg-adjusted SMR P value across datasets was at most 0.05 and the HEIDI P value was at least 0.05. Protein QTL Mendelian randomization and colocalization used the HipSci induced pluripotent stem cell proteome 56 , cerebrospinal fluid ApoE and BTN3A2 89 , plasma DPP4 90 , the UKB Pharma Proteomics Project 91 , and the dorsolateral prefrontal cortex proteome 92 , which was tested for all protein-coding genes within 500 kb of the loci with strong colocalization. A brain proteome-wide association study used FUSION weights 92, 93 (Table S19; Supplementary Note), and the protein QTL of each association was tested for colocalization with the edge signal (coloc.abf and SuSiE). Colocalization with MetaBrain eQTL of five brain tissues 52 used SuSiE on variants whose meta-analysis sample size was at least 90% of the gene maximum.

Target-chain audit. Each chain linked a locus, a candidate gene, and a colocalized trait (150 chains). The circuit link required closure above that of the other discovery loci (empirical P < 0.05); the disease link, SuSiE-confirmed colocalization; the gene link, a Tier 1 or Tier 2 gene or a brain protein QTL that colocalized with the edge; the causal link, a significant and directionally consistent cis-eQTL or protein QTL Mendelian randomization estimate; and the drug link, a drug or clinical candidate for the gene in the Open Targets Platform (release 26.09) 59 . A chain was complete when all five links passed and the estimated directions of effect agreed. The audit was then expanded with the 2,204 protein-coding genes within 500 kb of the loci, cell type-specific and PsychENCODE expression QTL 50, 51 , and GWAS of migraine, headache, stroke, and ischemic stroke. The re-scored audit comprised 295 chains (Supplementary Note). Clinical-stage compounds were matched to the colocalized trait by their Open Targets indications. Psychiatric indications were schizophrenia or psychosis, bipolar disorder, depression, anxiety, ADHD, autism, obsessive-compulsive or post-traumatic stress disorder, substance use, eating disorders, insomnia, Tourette syndrome, and agitation or delirium, each with its highest clinical stage in Open Targets.

Display items.

Display details of the main figures, including color scales, exclusions, and tie-breaking rules, are given in the Supplementary Note.

Footprints of all loci, edge-level SNP heritability and edge-level genetic correlation maps for 36 traits will be deposited in Zenodo on publication. External GWAS summary statistics are listed in Tables S15, S17, and S18, and the QTL datasets and the GWAS added in the expanded audit are described in the Supplementary Note. The table lists the genes at colocalized loci with at least one of four kinds of evidence. Gene link: APOE (protein-altering ε4 variant and colocalization with the cerebrospinal fluid ApoE protein QTL; APOE2 gene therapy and anti-amyloid antibodies act on this pathway) and genes whose brain expression QTL (PsychENCODE) colocalized with the edge at a locus colocalized with the trait (CRHR1, MAPT, and MAPKAPK5). Same trait: a compound had been tested for the colocalized trait. Cognitive impairment: a compound had been tested for cognitive impairment at a locus colocalized with cognitive traits. Psychiatric: a compound had a psychiatric indication at a locus colocalized with a psychiatric trait; for genes with only this evidence, the indication and highest clinical stage are given. Genes at one locus with the same evidence share a row. The kidney-function compounds of DDB1 (myeloma trials) are not counted. At 17q21.31, an inversion region with long-range linkage disequilibrium, the expression QTL of four genes colocalized. Without a gene link, the gene-locus link is positional. CP, cognitive performance; EA, educational attainment; eGFR, estimated glomerular filtration rate; Int., intelligence; MDD, major depressive disorder; MHC, major histocompatibility complex; RT, reaction time; SCZ, schizophrenia; T2D, type 2 diabetes.

METHODS

Imaging data. We used the resting and task fMRI data of the UKB, which obtained ethics approval from the North West Multicentre Research Ethics Committee (approval number: 11/NW/0382), and all participants provided written informed consent. Image acquisition and preprocessing are described in the Supplementary Note. The task fMRI used the emotion-processing task 71, 74 , and connectivity was computed over all task conditions. The preprocessed time series on the cortical surface were parcellated into the 360 areas of the Glasser atlas 1 . The functional connectivity between each pair of areas was computed with FSLNets 75 as the full correlation of their time series, converted by Fisher's r-to-z transformation and scaled as in the UKB imaging pipeline 76 . The 64,620 upper-triangular edges were analyzed as traits, and the areas were grouped into 12 functional networks 30 (Table S1).

Samples.

The discovery samples were drawn from the 56,614 and 47,976 UKB participants with resting and task fMRI from imaging phases 1 to 6. We included participants of self-reported white ethnic background (hereafter European ancestry) and excluded those flagged by the UKB as outliers for heterozygosity or missing rate, with sex-chromosome aneuploidy, or with discordant genetic and reported sex. After removal of participants with missing data, 53,096 (resting) and 45,115 (task) participants remained, of whom 52,983 and 45,010 were included in the association analyses, depending on the part of the connectome. The replication sample comprised 15,546 white British participants with resting fMRI from imaging phase 7, none of whom were in the discovery sample (Supplementary Note).

(A) SNP heritability of the 61,930 edges with estimates in both states (resting fMRI, n = 52,983; task fMRI, n = 45,010). Hexagons are shaded by edge count (log scale); dashed line, equal heritability; Rest > task, percentage of edges more heritable at rest. (B) Numbers of loci colocalizing with each complex trait 35 at PPH4 ≥ 0.8 and PPH4 > PPH3 (dark segments) or 0.5 < PPH4 < 0.8 (light segments), among all 247 resting and 96 task loci, for the 21 traits tested at all loci. The 12 traits with the most colocalizing resting loci are shown individually, and the other 9 are pooled (locus-trait pairs). Inset, locus-trait pairs (total, PPH4 > 0.5). (C) Percentage of the 64,620 edges with at least one significant association (P < 0.05/(M eff × K); K, features per layer) with clinical biomarkers, plasma proteins, or metabolomic measures, with the numbers of associated features and edge-feature pairs (log scale).
